104:         width = _gather_width(plan, m.gather_tensor) if m.kind == "gather" else ""
105:         lines.append(f"      // arrive {ev} via {m.text!r} ({sc})")
106:         has_dynamic_consumers = any(k[0] == ev for k in plan.ev_consumers)
107:         # DEVICE scope: last-arriver flush per domain (one L2 write-back per domain per coordinate)
108:         shares = {v for row in ep.share.values() for v in row if v > 0}
109:         nf = "_nf" if ep.producers_coherent else ""        # all producers store coherently: the payload is already past L2
110:         if ep.scope == Scope.DEVICE and not ep.runtime_init and len(shares) == 1:
111:             arrive = f"etx_arrive_flush_DEVICE_s{nf}(p, {ep.offset} + (idx), domain, {next(iter(shares))})"   # share as an immediate
112:         elif ep.scope == Scope.DEVICE:
113:             arrive = f"etx_arrive_flush_DEVICE{nf}(p, {ep.offset} + (idx), domain)"
114:         else:
115:             arrive = f"etx_arrive_{sc}(p.events + ETX_EVW({ep.offset} + (idx)))"
116:         if not has_dynamic_consumers:       # the producer's own mode is irrelevant: a static producer must still push dynamic consumers
117:             lines.append(f"#define ETX_TARGET(idx) (void){arrive}")
118:         else:
119:             lines.append(f"#define ETX_TARGET(idx) do {{ if ({arrive} == 0) etx_push_consumers(p, {ev_id}, (idx), domain); }} while (0)")
120:         lines.append(m.to_c(coord_vars, f"p.ev_shape[{ev_id}]", "ETX_TARGET", _runtime_ptrs(plan, g), width, _symbols(plan)).rstrip())
121:         lines.append("#undef ETX_TARGET")
122:     return "\n".join(lines)

52:   const int32_t share = p.ev_share ? p.ev_share[(size_t)domain * p.event_words + idx] : 0;
53:   if (share <= 1) { ETX_RELEASE_DEVICE(); return ETX_ARRIVE_DEVICE(p.events + ETX_EVW(idx)) - 1; }
54:   ETX_RELEASE_DOMAIN();                                              // drain this workgroup's stores into the domain's L2

[58] docs/DEP-GAP-LEDGER-2026-10-11.md:140 docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:64-72 => docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:64-72
CLAIM: The one-head owner protocol of `docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:64-72` joins prep, the five attention
64: ### The implementable protocol: one prep owner, early publication, last partition reduces
65:
66: Keep the current head-affine parallel partitions. Assign an owner for each `(step, layer, kv_head)`, with exactly one prep execution. The owner performs the existing q/k norm and RoPE, preserves its bf16 rounding and writes the current KV row once. After **all owner waves' stores have drained**, publish a head-local, epoch-qualified `prep_ready`. Publish it **before** the owner executes its attention partition, not at the end of the whole task. The other partitions acquire that publication and run their unchanged imported attention calls in parallel. The owner also runs its own partition.
67:
68: Each partition writes the original bf16/fp32 partial buffers, drains all producing waves and arrives at a per-head completion counter. The last arrival executes the original imported reduce for that head. Election changes the worker, not the reduce's partition order. It must acquire visibility for **all** producers' partials before reduction; last arrival alone does not make those data loads safe. Publish the normal reduced-output event only after all reduce waves finish. The existing o_proj consumers then proceed.
69:
70: This directly joins prep to the attention call and attention to reduce in the task body; it requires a new adapter/runtime protocol for **mid-body signals**, not a generic `pure` prologue fold. Import-round barriers must finish uniformly before reuse of a slot or before reduction. Static queues must place every producer before any blocked successor; a resident producer cannot be queued behind a waiting consumer on the same worker. A waiter must never monopolise the only execution resources its producer needs. Use discovered XCD identity, not block-index assumptions (`etx/machine/arch/gfx942.yaml:62,94,100`).
71:
72: Norms surrounding this segment are part of the same audit: the input/residual norm is already folded into qkv, and the post-attention residual norm into router (`model.py:299-305`; `etx/ops/gemv.py:81-85`). Keep those folds and rounding points; do not book their removed launches again. The remaining imported input/final RMSNorm can be chained only with a consumer that fits the same ownership and visibility contract. A qkv or o_proj grid distributed across XCDs still needs its true fan-in/fan-out dependency. The isolated residual norm opportunity has **[U] zero separate credit**; it is not another attention boundary.

[77] docs/DEP-GAP-LEDGER-2026-10-11.md:213 docs/FLOORS-2026-10-08.md:76-88 => docs/FLOORS-2026-10-08.md:76-88
CLAIM: 0.50 us per coherent trip from `docs/FLOORS-2026-10-08.md:76-88`).
76: | Coherent round trip past L2 | 0.50 to 0.69 us | `docs/TILES-2026-10-07.md:32`, `hw_cohload.log`: coherent span 0.50 us |
77: | Cached round trip, L1 or scalar cache | 0.104 us | `docs/VALIDATION-baremetal-2026-10-01.md:117` |
78: | Handoff | 9.02 us per layer | `docs/BUDGET-2026-10-01.md:161`, 9 edges |
79: | Stream probe | achievable column | `docs/BUDGET-2026-10-01.md:133-141` |
80:
81: The probe divides the bytes of one task by the hw_stream rate of one workgroup. The rate at 256 workgroups is an interpolation. Source: `docs/BUDGET-2026-10-01.md:86-90`.
82: The `hw_cohload.log` median of one workgroup is 0.28 us. The 0.50 us value is the span over all workgroups.
83:
84: Each hop class has one price:
85: 1. A coherent load (`sc1`) and the drain of a coherent store cost one coherent round trip.
86: 2. A plain load costs the cached price. After an acquire (`buffer_inv sc1`), a plain load misses L2 and costs one coherent round trip. attn, reduce, in_norm and argmax start after an acquire.
87: 3. A scalar load, a scratch reload and the drain of a plain store cost the cached price.
88: 4. An LDS step costs nothing in the floor. The tables report it separately.

[94] docs/DEP-GAP-LEDGER-2026-10-11.md:266 docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:144 => docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:144
CLAIM: microbenchmark: **15 min = $0.4975 [E]** (`docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:144`), threshold 1.042 us/layer
144: The strongest argument is architectural, not a bandwidth impossibility theorem. Current MoE work remains skeleton-bound after the easy machinery fixes; down, o_proj, lm_head, publication and dispatcher terms are already closed. Fixed imported attention/reduce work remains. The earlier all-phases-at-floor sum was **1.171 ms [E]**, only `1.171 - 1.137667 = 0.033333 ms [E]` headroom (`docs/LEVERS-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:47`). Floors omit real MFMA/VALU/LDS work. Keeping imported bodies and allowing only the existing KEY32 selection removes `0.125 + 0.123 + (0.324 - 0.231) = 0.341 ms [E]` from that fantasy. Adding even the full `0.096 ms [E]` boundary allowance gives `1.171 - 0.341 + 0.096 = 0.926 ms [E]`; this still includes `0.014 + 0.012 = 0.026 ms [E]` of near-floor passengers that this plan closes. Its practical ceiling is the table's **0.900 ms [E]**, conditional on the transfer above. There is no evidence that the missing retained work can disappear while preserving exact imported outputs and greedy tokens.

84: Gate the protocol first with a small multi-partition payload test, byte-for-byte original imported calls, repeated cache-line reuse and epoch rollover. 67:
68: Each partition writes the original bf16/fp32 partial buffers, drains all producing waves and arrives at a per-head completion counter. [102] docs/DEP-GAP-LEDGER-2026-10-11.md:294 etx/sim/xcd.py:1-20 => etx/sim/xcd.py:1-20
CLAIM: at this plan's granularity; a same-bodies resimulated schedule (`etx/sim/xcd.py:1-20` exists for exactly
1: """Bandwidth-aware simulation of a compiled plan on the XCDs of one GPU.
2:
3: Where etx.sim.protocol gives every task a fixed duration, this one gives it a cost *shape* and lets the tasks that run at
4: the same time share the memory system, so what one XCD does changes what another gets:
5:
6:   task = wait (its input events, each fired at its last producer's end + the release, seen after the hand-off of the
7:          event's scope) -> front + floor + etx (fixed: the hardware minimum a workgroup pays before its bytes, plus
8:          ETX's own acquire / argument / release cost) -> chain (fixed: the kernel's dependent global round trips,
9:          barriers, reductions) -> stream (bytes, fluid: rate-limited per CU and sharing the GPU's bandwidth) -> end
10:
11: Since 2026-09-30 the fixed part is dominated by `floor_us` and `etx_us`, measured per phase rather than derived from
12: an instruction count: hw_bench stream shows 296 workgroups cost about 3 us even when they read 896 B each, and the
13: measured builds put ETX's own share on top of that (docs/MODEL-vllm-kernels.md section 19). The earlier model
14: charged a per-task tax of about 2 us and counted events, and predicted gains from folding that the hardware did not
15: pay: build 2 5.934 ms -> build 3 5.895 ms, against a predicted 0.9 ms.
16:
17: Streams follow Little's law: all of them see one loaded latency L = max(idle round trip, bytes in flight on the GPU /
18: bandwidth), and each pulls its bytes in flight / (L + the work of a trip that is not overlapped with memory). So a GPU
19: full of streams is bandwidth-bound, a few streams are latency-bound, and an XCD that streams alone runs faster. Workers run their static queue in order, one task at a time (the generated kernel's loop).
20:

[184] docs/XCD-TIMELINE-2026-10-11.md:281 docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/latency.txt:5-14 => docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/latency.txt:5-14
CLAIM: The per-phase bandwidth shares above divide each phase's achieved GB/s by the whole device's 4.3 TB/s. No phase runs on all eight XCDs. `latency.txt` already measures the per-phase `XCDs live` column (`docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/latency.txt:5-14`). One XCD's share of the achievable 4.3 TB/s is `4.3 / 8 = 0.5375 TB/s` [E], so a phase's own ceiling is `XCDs live x 0.5375 TB/s`, and its share of that ceiling is the only bandwidth number that means anything for it. A phase on four XCDs could never have reached 4.3 TB/s; comparing it to 4.3 TB/s makes latency-bound phases look like they waste bandwidth they never had.

[192] docs/XCD-TIMELINE-2026-10-11.md:324 account.txt:8-9 => docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/account.txt:8-9
CLAIM: One precision about what "idle" means here. `any body` marks an XCD busy when at least one of its 38 workers is in a task body. At worker granularity the busy share is lower: bodies cover 40.03% of worker-time (bytes 27.57% + machinery 12.46%, `account.txt:8-9`), and 53.00% of worker-time is dependency wait (`account.txt:6-7`). A worker in a dependency wait has already taken its next task and still occupies its CU; the 27.06% counts XCDs with no body at all, where all 38 workers are waiting or done. Both numbers matter below: the first says how much work could in principle be re-issued, the second says how much of the machine has nothing issued at all.
8:   bytes      (a)      369807.6 worker-us   27.57%
9:   machinery  (c)      167084.9 worker-us   12.46%

CLAIM: **L2 (4 MB per XCD, private).** A prefetch lands in the L2 of the XCD that issues it, so an L2 window only works when prefetcher and consumer are the same XCD. What fits per XCD: qkv, o_proj, moe_down and everything smaller; moe_up (6.29 MB) and a whole layer (14.5 MB) do not. Fleet hit this cliff on the same GPU: making 113 MB of weights cacheable pushed the working set past the 4 MB per-XCD L2 and ran 18% slower (fleet `docs/STATUS.md:81,233`).

[200] docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:36 account.txt:72 => docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/account.txt:72
CLAIM: **The window is one layer, and what governs it is the 256 MB MALL minus the 104 MB resident KV cache.** In L2 the window is at most one moe_down-sized phase per XCD, governed by the 4 MB cliff. One phase-specific limit governs separately: moe_up's *selected* weights are 50.33 MB, but which experts are selected is only known when topk finishes, 1.08 us (median) before moe_up starts (`account.txt:72`). Prefetching all 128 experts instead of the 8 selected is `50.33 x 128 / 8 = 805.3 MB` per layer [E] (`examples/llm/configs/qwen3-30b-a3b.json:1`: 128 experts, 8 per token), 5.3 times the MALL headroom. moe_up is therefore not prefetchable in practice; its window collapses to the 1.08 us topk-to-moe_up gap.

[205] docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:46 examples/vllm_llm/build.sh:52-55 => examples/vllm_llm/build.sh:52-55
CLAIM: - Today's consumer weight loads are non-temporal by default (`examples/vllm_llm/build.sh:52-55`, `etx/ops/gemv.py:124-126`): they are chosen so the 6.08 GB weight stream does not evict the MALL-resident KV cache. Whether an NT consumer load can even *hit* a prefetched MALL line is [U]. If it cannot, the consumer phases must switch to cached loads, which is exactly the change fleet measured at 18% slower when applied broadly (fleet `docs/STATUS.md:81`).
52: # ETX_VL_NT: weight loads in the DSL GEMV marked non-temporal (etx/ops/gemv.py dsl_wload). ON by default since
53: # 2026-10-01 round 16: weights are read once per token and have no reuse, so caching them evicts the MALL-resident
54: # KV cache and the activations. Measured -0.106 ms/token with L2 hit rate 31.9% -> 34.6%; ETX_VL_NT=0 restores it.
55: NTF=(); [ "${ETX_VL_NT:-1}" = 1 ] && NTF=(-DETX_DSL_NT)

[208] docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:49 results_2026-10-01/round15/cache.txt:3-5 => results_2026-10-01/round15/cache.txt:3-5
CLAIM: **The eviction risk, stated plainly:** if a prefetched line is evicted before the consumer reads it, the line is read from DRAM twice — once by the prefetcher, once by the consumer — and the step is slower, not faster, by the prefetch's own traffic. The experiment detects this two ways: (a) `rocprofv3`'s `TCC_EA0_RDREQ_DRAM_sum` per dispatch, compared against the control arm; the baseline is 48.9-50.9 M requests at 119.5 B per request, one fetch per weight byte (`results_2026-10-01/round15/cache.txt:3-5`, `ntc.txt:1-3`, `results_2026-10-01/round15/FINDING.md:55-56`) [M], so any excess over the known 6.19 GB is re-reads; (b) the pass-C rate in the survival arm of the microbenchmark (section 5), which falls back to the cold rate if the window was evicted.
3: TCC_EA0_RDREQ_DRAM_sum           n=1055  avg=      50867625.8 sum=       53665345208
4: TCC_HIT_sum                      n=1055  avg=      23838256.2 sum=       25149360297
5: TCC_MISS_sum                     n=1055  avg=      50927282.9 sum=       53728283499

[211] docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:53 account.txt:82-143 => docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/account.txt:82-143
CLAIM: **Compute: no pair of phases is independent.** The decoder layer is a serial chain, qkv -> prep -> attn -> reduce -> o_proj -> router -> topk -> moe_up -> moe_down -> next layer's qkv (the layer order of `account.txt:82-143`), and every consecutive pair carries a measured dependency edge in the trace (`account.txt:62-79`). The brief's example needs a correction here: layer N's `attn` and `reduce` are indeed dependent, but layer N's `moe_down` and layer N+1's `qkv` are **not** independent either — the trace measures 47 such edges and 190.2 us of gaps on them (`account.txt:63,65`) [M], because qkv_{N+1} reads the residual stream that moe_down_N produces (the input/residual norm is folded into qkv). What is independent of everything is the **weight stream**: weights are constants, so any phase's weights can be loaded during any earlier phase, subject to capacity (section 1) and to the addresses being known (moe_up/moe_down only after topk).
82: layer 24: which XCD runs which phase, us from the step start

CLAIM: Why the realised value will be lower than even 63 us: (a) the +9% was measured with no co-running consumer stream; during P2/P3 the prefetch competes with the running phase for the same HBM, and the measured price of filling idle bandwidth next to a latency chain is a doubling of its round trip, 1.52 -> 3.08 us (`overlap.log:15-16`, split placement, background 4.31 TB/s) — the latency phases carry 396.4 us of exposed hops [E], so even a 10% slowdown there (~40 us) eats most of the central estimate; (b) the MALL window sits at 86% occupancy with the KV cache, so some eviction is likely and every evicted line is read twice (section 2); (c) if NT consumer loads cannot hit the prefetched lines [U], the consumers must go cacheable, risking the fleet 18% L2-cliff loss; (d) fleet already measured this mechanism's two direct ancestors on the same GPU: idle-worker prefetch into the Infinity Cache gave no gain, and prefetch across the event wait was 4% slower (fleet `docs/STATUS.md:72,30`).

CLAIM: 1. **The idle is a symptom, not a resource.** A worker idles because its next task's inputs do not exist yet. Section 3 shows there is no independent compute to give it: every consecutive phase pair is dependency-linked in the trace, including the pair the brief calls independent (moe_down_N -> qkv_{N+1}, 47 measured edges, `account.txt:63,65`). The only thing that can move into the idle time is weight traffic, and weight traffic's value is bounded by the cache-rate delta, measured at +9% at layer size and negative at window size (`overlap.log:3-8`).

[232] docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:125 docs/OPTIMISATIONS.md:27 => docs/OPTIMISATIONS.md:27
CLAIM: The evidence that settles it, in order: the existing `-DETX_NO_TOUCH` A/B (a 20-second existing-switch probe that prices the mechanism's already-shipped within-phase form, `docs/OPTIMISATIONS.md:27`); phase 1's S1/S2 (does a prefetched window survive the step's own NT traffic at all) and S3/S4/I1 (is there a benefit, and what does it cost the latency chain); then phase 2's KEEP vote. If S1 or S2 fails, the idle time is provably not usable by this mechanism at this model shape, and the counter-argument holds. If phase 1 passes and phase 2 still misses KEEP, the idle time is usable in principle but worth less than 0.030 ms — and the 300 tok/s question must be answered elsewhere.
27: | `ETX_VL_TOUCH` | **universal (unvalidated)** | — | On in every good build, but its value was never isolated *after* `ETX_VL_COH` changed what survives a phase boundary. A 20-second A/B would settle it. |

[254] docs/MOE-BODY-ANATOMY-2026-10-11.md:44 gemv.py:174-184,773-777 => etx/ops/gemv.py:174-184,773-777
CLAIM: The path is one **combined GU1 pass** for up, not two streams in succession. Up: `SEG=64×8=512`, `spr=2048/512=4`, `nrt=64`, `S=256`, `per=256/16=16`, `sb=16×wave`, `se=sb+16 [E]` (`gemv.py:174-184,773-777`). Waves before the gate/up split read the gate base; waves after it add the gap `(768-32)×2048` bf16 elements (`:188-196,777`). Each wave consumes **four complete rows [E]**. Its default pipeline has **U=2**, **eight trips**, each issuing two **16 B/lane** loads, `16 waves×64 lanes×2×16 = 32768 B` in flight/task [E] (`:240-248,354-359`). This is half the old full-depth pipeline; do not use the comment's old 64 KB as today's depth.

347: // Half in-flight depth (8 dwords per lane) is the DEFAULT since round 34: 4.832 against 4.884 wall ms/token for
348: // the full depth (3 interleaved runs each, same box), VGPR/spill/occupancy identical (128/8/4) -- the win is VMEM
349: // queue pressure and 1 MB less code, not registers. Round 32's Little's-law headroom at full depth was 4.8x, so
350: // half still covers the latency 2.4x over. Occupancy itself CANNOT move here: the persistent kernel launches one
351: // 16-wave workgroup per CU, so 4 waves/SIMD is fixed by the grid, not by VGPRs -- forcing VGPR 64 via launch
352: // bounds left runtime occupancy at 4.000 and cost 2x in spill traffic (9.39 wall, 1678 spills). -DETX_DSL_U4
353: // restores the old depth.

[270] docs/MOE-BODY-ANATOMY-2026-10-11.md:61 G:389-407,763 / 821 => etx/ops/gemv.py:389-407,763 / 821
CLAIM: | D. Stage activation bf16 → fp32 LDS, then stage barrier | G:389-407,763 / 821; coh.py:41,47-51 | Up/down read 2048×2=4096 / 768×2=1536 B [E]. Two `global_load_dwordx2 sc0 sc1` per active lane, 256/96 active lanes; unpack shifts/ands, two `ds_write_b128` per active lane. LDS write 8192/3072 B → 0.030476/0.011429 us nominal [E]. One coherent group latency, not two serial pointer-chase hops. Stage rendezvous adds bare 0.0612 us [E using impbar_hw.log:4] plus producer skew/drain [U]. | I | [U] / [U] |
389: static __device__ __forceinline__ void dsl_stage(const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ x, int K, float* xs) {   // bf16 -> fp32 LDS, 16 B per thread
390: #ifdef DSL_ABLATE_STAGE
391:   __syncthreads(); return;          // ablation: the barrier without the fill (the dot then reads stale LDS: WRONG output)
392: #endif
393: #ifdef ETX_DSL_COH
394:   for (int i = (int)threadIdx.x; i < K / 8; i += blockDim.x) {
395:     // activation: two device-coherent 8 B loads (both stay in flight; the compiler places the waits)
396:     const unsigned long long lo = dsl_a_ld64((const unsigned long long*)(x + 8 * i));
397:     const unsigned long long hi = dsl_a_ld64((const unsigned long long*)(x + 8 * i) + 1);
398:     dsl_u32x4 v; v[0] = (unsigned)(lo & 0xffffffffull); v[1] = (unsigned)(lo >> 32); v[2] = (unsigned)(hi & 0xffffffffull); v[3] = (unsigned)(hi >> 32);
399: #else
400:   const DSL_G dsl_u32x4* xv = (const DSL_G dsl_u32x4*)x;
401:   for (int i = (int)threadIdx.x; i < K / 8; i += blockDim.x) {
402:     const dsl_u32x4 v = xv[i];
403: #endif
404: #pragma unroll
405:     for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { xs[8 * i + 2 * j] = __uint_as_float(v[j] << 16); xs[8 * i + 2 * j + 1] = __uint_as_float(v[j] & 0xffff0000u); }
406:   }
407:   __syncthreads();
763:   dsl_stage(d.x + (size_t)rq * d.sx, d.K, xs);
821:   dsl_stage(d.x + (size_t)rq * d.sx + (size_t)s * d.K, d.K, xs);

47: static __device__ __forceinline__ dsl_u32x4 dsl_a_ld128(const __hip_bfloat16* p) {
48:   const unsigned long long lo = dsl_a_ld64((const unsigned long long*)p);
49:   const unsigned long long hi = dsl_a_ld64((const unsigned long long*)p + 1);

275: ## 8. Corrections of 2026-10-11, second pass
276:
277: Two choices in this report were wrong. This section corrects both. Nothing above is deleted; the affected lines carry a bracketed pointer here. Both corrections are CPU-only recomputations of the same artefacts; no GPU ran for them.
278:
279: ### 8.1 The denominator: each phase has its own ceiling
280:
281: The per-phase bandwidth shares above divide each phase's achieved GB/s by the whole device's 4.3 TB/s. No phase runs on all eight XCDs. `latency.txt` already measures the per-phase `XCDs live` column (`docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/latency.txt:5-14`). One XCD's share of the achievable 4.3 TB/s is `4.3 / 8 = 0.5375 TB/s` [E], so a phase's own ceiling is `XCDs live x 0.5375 TB/s`, and its share of that ceiling is the only bandwidth number that means anything for it. A phase on four XCDs could never have reached 4.3 TB/s; comparing it to 4.3 TB/s makes latency-bound phases look like they waste bandwidth they never had.
282:
283: Corrected table, recomputed from the JSON artefacts at full precision (`latency.json`, `bandwidth.json`; the rounded text columns are `latency.txt:5-15` and `bandwidth.txt:3-15`). "Ceiling" is `XCDs live x 0.5375 TB/s` [E]. "Own share" is `achieved GB/s / ceiling` [E]. The old column, the share of the whole-device 4.3 TB/s, is kept so the size of the correction is visible.
284:
285: | Phase | XCDs live [M] | Own ceiling TB/s [E] | Achieved GB/s [M] | **Share of own ceiling [E]** | Old: % of 4.3 TB/s [E] |
286: |---|---|---|---|---|---|
287: | lm_head | 7.98 | 4.288 | 3881.6 | **90.5%** | 90.3% |
288: | moe_up | 7.67 | 4.120 | 2713.6 | **65.9%** | 63.1% |

[319] docs/MOE-BODY-ANATOMY-2026-10-11.md:147 G:168-278 => etx/ops/gemv.py:168-278
CLAIM: In **`dsl_gemv_rows_nb` (G:168-278)**, add a fixed whole-row-owned path:
168: static __device__ __forceinline__ void dsl_gemv_rows_nb(const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ W, int K, int KS, const float* xs, int r0, int r1, float* part, Epi epi
169: #ifdef ETX_DSL_GU1
170:                                                         , int gsplit = 0, long long ggap = 0
171: #endif
172:                                                         ) {
173:   typedef typename dsl_wvec<EPL>::T V;
174:   constexpr int SEG = ETX_WAVE_SIZE * EPL;
175:   const dsl_lds_f* xl = (const dsl_lds_f*)xs;
176:   dsl_lds_fw* pl = (dsl_lds_fw*)part;
177: #ifdef ETX_DSL_SCALAR                                    // all of these are the same in every lane: say so to the compiler
178:   K = etx_uniform(K); r0 = etx_uniform(r0); r1 = etx_uniform(r1);
179:   const int lane = etx_lane(), wv = etx_uniform(etx_wave()), nw = etx_nwaves(), nrt = r1 - r0;
180: #else
181:   const int lane = etx_lane(), wv = etx_wave(), nw = etx_nwaves(), nrt = r1 - r0;
182: #endif
183:   const int spr = K / SEG, S = nrt * spr;
184:   const int per = (S + nw - 1) / nw, sb = wv * per, se = min(S, sb + per);
185:   for (int i = (int)threadIdx.x; i < nw * nrt * NB; i += blockDim.x) pl[i] = 0.f;
186:   __syncthreads();
187: #if defined(ETX_DSL_SCALAR) || defined(ETX_DSL_GU1)
188:   const DSL_G V* ubase = (const DSL_G V*)(W + (size_t)r0 * K
189: #ifdef ETX_DSL_GU1
190:                                           + ((gsplit > 0 && sb >= gsplit * spr) ? ggap : 0)
191: #endif
192:                                           );
193: #ifdef DSL_ABLATE_NOVMEM
194:   const DSL_G V* base = ubase + lane;
195: #endif
196: #define DSL_WP(sg) (ubase + (size_t)(sg) * ETX_WAVE_SIZE + lane)   // uniform part first: one scalar base per trip
197: #else
198:   const DSL_G V* base = (const DSL_G V*)(W + (size_t)r0 * K) + lane;
199: #define DSL_WP(sg) (base + (size_t)(sg) * ETX_WAVE_SIZE)
200: #endif
201:   int cur = sb / spr;
202:   float acc[NB];
203: #pragma unroll
204:   for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) acc[b] = 0.f;
205:   auto flush = [&]() {
206: #ifndef DSL_ABLATE_NORED                                 // ablation: no per-row wave_sum, no partial writes (WRONG output)
207: #pragma unroll
208: #ifdef ETX_DSL_WSUM
209:     for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) { const float v = dsl_wave_sum0(acc[b]); if (lane == 0) pl[(wv * nrt + cur) * NB + b] += v; acc[b] = 0.f; }
210: #else
211:     for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) { const float v = etx_wave_sum(acc[b]); if (lane == 0) pl[(wv * nrt + cur) * NB + b] += v; acc[b] = 0.f; }
212: #endif
213: #endif
214:   };
215: #ifdef ETX_DSL_SCALAR
216:   // (row, segment) advanced by one per consumed vector: segments are consumed in order, so this is sg / spr and
217:   // sg % spr without the division (dsl_gemv_accum's walk, minus its second reduction)
218:   int row = cur, seg = sb - cur * spr;
219:   auto consume = [&](int, V w) {
220:     if (row != cur) { flush(); cur = row; }
221:     const int off = seg * SEG + lane * EPL;
222: #pragma unroll
223:     for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) acc[b] += dsl_dot(w, xl + b * KS + off);
224:     if (++seg == spr) { seg = 0; ++row; }
225:   };
226: #else
227:   auto consume = [&](int sg, V w) {
228:     const int row = sg / spr;
229:     if (row != cur) { flush(); cur = row; }
230:     const int off = (sg - row * spr) * SEG + lane * EPL;
231: #pragma unroll
232:     for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) acc[b] += dsl_dot(w, xl + b * KS + off);   // one load, NB dots: the point of the batch
233:   };
234: #endif

117:     // etx_wave_sum's butterfly (partners at lane distance 32, 16, 8, 4, 2, 1) with only lane 0's result defined, so each
118:     // step need be exact only on the lanes lane 0 later reads: 32 by ds_bpermute, 16 by ds_swizzle's xor mode (exact in
119:     // each 32-lane half), 8 by DPP row_ror:8 (= xor 8 inside a 16-lane row), 4 by row_shl:4 (lane i reads i + 4: exact
120:     // on lanes 0-3, all the later steps read), 2 and 1 by quad_perm. Every lane lane 0 depends on adds the same two
121:     // values as in etx_wave_sum, and fp32 addition commutes, so lane 0 is bit-identical; the other lanes hold partial
122:     // sums. Replaces four of six LDS-pipe round trips and the per-step lane-index arithmetic of __shfl_xor.
123:     static_assert(ETX_WAVE_SIZE == 64, "dsl_wave_sum0 is written for wave64");
124:     static __device__ __forceinline__ float dsl_wave_sum0(float v) {
125:       v += __int_as_float(__builtin_amdgcn_ds_bpermute((etx_lane() ^ 32) << 2, __float_as_int(v)));
126:       v += __int_as_float(__builtin_amdgcn_ds_swizzle(__float_as_int(v), 0x401f));                  // and 0x1f, xor 0x10
127:       v += __int_as_float(__builtin_amdgcn_update_dpp(0, __float_as_int(v), 0x128, 0xf, 0xf, false)); // row_ror:8
128:       v += __int_as_float(__builtin_amdgcn_update_dpp(0, __float_as_int(v), 0x104, 0xf, 0xf, false)); // row_shl:4
129:       v += __int_as_float(__builtin_amdgcn_update_dpp(0, __float_as_int(v), 0x4e, 0xf, 0xf, false));  // quad_perm [2,3,0,1]
130:       v += __int_as_float(__builtin_amdgcn_update_dpp(0, __float_as_int(v), 0xb1, 0xf, 0xf, false));  // quad_perm [1,0,3,2]

28: struct Bf16ProbeRecord {
29:   unsigned long long begin, end;
30:   unsigned task, wave, xcd, row;
31: };
32: struct Bf16Probe {
33:   unsigned long long begin = 0, end = 0;
34:   bool selected, started = false, ended = false;
35:   // compiler barrier bounds issue placement, without retiring memory operations
36:   static __device__ __forceinline__ unsigned long long tick() {
37:     unsigned long long t;
38:     asm volatile("s_memrealtime %0" : "=s"(t) :: "memory");
39:     return t;
40:   }
41:   __device__ __forceinline__ void first() { if (selected && !started) { begin = tick(); started = true; } }
42:   __device__ __forceinline__ void last() { if (selected && started && !ended) { end = tick(); ended = true; } }
43:   __device__ __forceinline__ void save(Bf16ProbeRecord* out, unsigned task) {
44:     if (selected && threadIdx.x % 64 == 0) {
45:       *out = {begin, end, task, (unsigned)etx_wave(), ETX_DOMAIN_ID(), BF16_PROBE_ROW};
46:     }

[368] docs/MOE-BODY-ANATOMY-2026-10-11.md:205 pairs.py:138-163 => examples/vllm_llm/pairs.py:138-163
138: def verdict(gains: list[float]) -> dict:
139:     med = median(gains)
140:     spread = max(gains) - min(gains)
141:     positive = all(g > 0 for g in gains)
142:     ok = positive and med > MIN_GAIN and med > SPREAD_FACTOR * spread
143:     return dict(median=med, spread=spread, positive=positive, established=ok)
144:
145:
146: def report(ps: list[tuple[Run, Run]], log: Log, basis: str, out=None) -> bool:
147:     out = out or sys.stdout
148:     gains = {"wall": [], "device": []}
149:     print(f"{'pair':>4} {'fused wall':>10} {'graph wall':>10} {'gain':>8}   {'fused dev':>9} {'graph dev':>9} {'gain':>8}",
150:           file=out)
151:     for i, (f, g) in enumerate(ps, 1):
152:         gw = (g.wall_ms - f.wall_ms) / g.wall_ms
153:         gd = (g.device_ms - f.device_ms) / g.device_ms
154:         gains["wall"].append(gw); gains["device"].append(gd)
155:         print(f"{i:4d} {f.wall_ms:10.3f} {g.wall_ms:10.3f} {gw:+8.2%}   {f.device_ms:9.3f} {g.device_ms:9.3f} {gd:+8.2%}", file=out)
156:     result = None
157:     for b in (basis, "device" if basis == "wall" else "wall"):
158:         v = verdict(gains[b])
159:         word = "ESTABLISHED" if v["established"] else "NOT ESTABLISHED"
160:         print(f"VERDICT {b} {word}: median gain {v['median']:+.2%} (needs > {MIN_GAIN:.0%}), "
161:               f"2 x spread {SPREAD_FACTOR * v['spread']:.2%} (needs < median), "
162:               f"every pair positive: {'yes' if v['positive'] else 'no'}"
163:               + ("" if b == basis else "  [secondary basis]"), file=out)

CLAIM: The control is already specialized in the ways the old ladder did not have: one gate/up pass, scalarized arguments/incremental segment walk, lane-0-only wave reduction, and reduced default load depth (`G:156-224,345-359,767-783`; flags recorded at `results_qwen300_2026-10-11/certificate_off/build_identity.json:23`). 159: static __device__ __forceinline__ float dsl_bfr(float v) { return __bfloat162float(__float2bfloat16(v)); }
160: static __device__ __forceinline__ float dsl_silu(float g) { return g / (1.f + __expf(-g)); }
161: // rows [r0, r1) of W (row-major bf16, K columns) times xs (fp32 in LDS); part: LDS, >= nwaves * (r1 - r0) floats;
162: // epi(row, dot) once per row by one thread. Deterministic: per-wave partials are summed in wave order.
163: // NB columns: the weight vector is loaded ONCE and dotted with NB staged activation vectors (column b at xs + b * KS),
164: // giving NB partials per (wave, row). NB = 1 is the batch-1 GEMV and collapses to the original indexing.
165: // ETX_DSL_GU1: rows [gsplit, r1 - r0) of the pass sit ggap elements further on (moe_up's up rows behind its gate rows),
166: // so gate and up stream as ONE pass. The caller guarantees no wave's segment range crosses gsplit (one base per wave).
167: template <int EPL, int U, int NB, class Epi>
168: static __device__ __forceinline__ void dsl_gemv_rows_nb(const __hip_bfloat16* __restrict__ W, int K, int KS, const float* xs, int r0, int r1, float* part, Epi epi
169: #ifdef ETX_DSL_GU1
170:                                                         , int gsplit = 0, long long ggap = 0
171: #endif
172:                                                         ) {
173:   typedef typename dsl_wvec<EPL>::T V;
174:   constexpr int SEG = ETX_WAVE_SIZE * EPL;
175:   const dsl_lds_f* xl = (const dsl_lds_f*)xs;
176:   dsl_lds_fw* pl = (dsl_lds_fw*)part;
177: #ifdef ETX_DSL_SCALAR                                    // all of these are the same in every lane: say so to the compiler
178:   K = etx_uniform(K); r0 = etx_uniform(r0); r1 = etx_uniform(r1);
179:   const int lane = etx_lane(), wv = etx_uniform(etx_wave()), nw = etx_nwaves(), nrt = r1 - r0;
180: #else
181:   const int lane = etx_lane(), wv = etx_wave(), nw = etx_nwaves(), nrt = r1 - r0;
182: #endif
183:   const int spr = K / SEG, S = nrt * spr;
184:   const int per = (S + nw - 1) / nw, sb = wv * per, se = min(S, sb + per);
185:   for (int i = (int)threadIdx.x; i < nw * nrt * NB; i += blockDim.x) pl[i] = 0.f;
186:   __syncthreads();
187: #if defined(ETX_DSL_SCALAR) || defined(ETX_DSL_GU1)
188:   const DSL_G V* ubase = (const DSL_G V*)(W + (size_t)r0 * K
189: #ifdef ETX_DSL_GU1
190:                                           + ((gsplit > 0 && sb >= gsplit * spr) ? ggap : 0)
191: #endif
192:                                           );
193: #ifdef DSL_ABLATE_NOVMEM
194:   const DSL_G V* base = ubase + lane;
195: #endif
196: #define DSL_WP(sg) (ubase + (size_t)(sg) * ETX_WAVE_SIZE + lane)   // uniform part first: one scalar base per trip
197: #else
198:   const DSL_G V* base = (const DSL_G V*)(W + (size_t)r0 * K) + lane;
199: #define DSL_WP(sg) (base + (size_t)(sg) * ETX_WAVE_SIZE)
200: #endif
201:   int cur = sb / spr;
202:   float acc[NB];
203: #pragma unroll
204:   for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) acc[b] = 0.f;
205:   auto flush = [&]() {
206: #ifndef DSL_ABLATE_NORED                                 // ablation: no per-row wave_sum, no partial writes (WRONG output)
207: #pragma unroll
208: #ifdef ETX_DSL_WSUM
209:     for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) { const float v = dsl_wave_sum0(acc[b]); if (lane == 0) pl[(wv * nrt + cur) * NB + b] += v; acc[b] = 0.f; }
210: #else
211:     for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) { const float v = etx_wave_sum(acc[b]); if (lane == 0) pl[(wv * nrt + cur) * NB + b] += v; acc[b] = 0.f; }
212: #endif
213: #endif
214:   };
215: #ifdef ETX_DSL_SCALAR
216:   // (row, segment) advanced by one per consumed vector: segments are consumed in order, so this is sg / spr and
217:   // sg % spr without the division (dsl_gemv_accum's walk, minus its second reduction)
218:   int row = cur, seg = sb - cur * spr;
219:   auto consume = [&](int, V w) {
220:     if (row != cur) { flush(); cur = row; }
221:     const int off = seg * SEG + lane * EPL;
222: #pragma unroll
223:     for (int b = 0; b < NB; ++b) acc[b] += dsl_dot(w, xl + b * KS + off);
224:     if (++seg == spr) { seg = 0; ++row; }
345: // K % 512 == 0: 16 B loads, 4 in flight per lane; K % 256 == 0: 8 B loads, 8 in flight: 64 KB per workgroup of 16 waves
346: // (the megakernel's 1024 threads; 16 per lane as examples/llm/gemv.h's 4-wave tiles spilled 359 VGPRs at 128 per lane)
347: // Half in-flight depth (8 dwords per lane) is the DEFAULT since round 34: 4.832 against 4.884 wall ms/token for
348: // the full depth (3 interleaved runs each, same box), VGPR/spill/occupancy identical (128/8/4) -- the win is VMEM
349: // queue pressure and 1 MB less code, not registers. Round 32's Little's-law headroom at full depth was 4.8x, so
350: // half still covers the latency 2.4x over. Occupancy itself CANNOT move here: the persistent kernel launches one

769:   // segment range both as two passes of n rows and as one pass of 2n (so each row is one wave's in-order sum either
770:   // way, plus exact zeros from the other waves), and no wave straddles gate and up. Saves the second pass's partial
771:   // zeroing, cross-wave reduction and two of its barriers. Qwen3-30B-A3B: n = 32, K = 2048: 8 then 16 segments per

150: wave. That is also the real reason two workgroups per CU measured slower
151: long ago: they could not be co-resident, so they serialised and each took

155: Sources: [ROCm occupancy math on CDNA](https://rocm.blogs.amd.com/software-tools-optimization/occupancy-math-mi355x/README.html),
156: [ROCm MI300X workload optimization](https://rocm.docs.amd.com/en/latest/how-to/rocm-for-ai/inference-optimization/workload.html),
157: [Databricks, LLM inference performance engineering](https://www.databricks.com/blog/llm-inference-performance-engineering-best-practices),
158: [MoE-Inference-Bench](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.17467).
159:
160: Raw numbers for the occupancy test are in
161: [`results/microbench_f_occupancy.txt`](../results/microbench_f_occupancy.txt).

[379] docs/MOE-BODY-ANATOMY-2026-10-11.md:263 docs/ETX_Technical_Design.md:971,1030,1042 => docs/ETX_Technical_Design.md:971,1030,1042
971: 4. **L2-warming prefetch of the task's own weights before its wait is a loss** (+2.5-3.5%, 64 or 128 KB per task): the extra loads compete with the previous phase's streaming tail. Cross-barrier prefetch needs LDS staging or a smaller, later window, not L2 warming.
1030: `buffer_inv sc0` does not invalidate the vector L1 here: a consumer re-reading lines it read before gets its own stale copy (64 B and 8 KB payloads stale in nearly every round, same XCD or uncached memory; 64 KB clean because it overflows L1). ETX's DOMAIN acquire and fleet's XCD-local acquire both used it; outputs stayed exact because tile bodies stream megabytes through L1 between reuses. The DOMAIN acquire is now `buffer_inv sc1` (cost: Qwen3-8B 6.14 vs 6.15 ms, none; DeepSeek-V2-Lite 3.78 vs 4.04 ms, 7%); `-DETX_ACQUIRE_DOMAIN_RELAXED` restores sc0 under the stated eviction condition. Even the cheapest correct whole-GPU barrier (5.5 us) costs 3.4 kernel boundaries.
1042: **Overlap (`bench/overlap/overlap_bench.hip`).** A 64 KB fold read by 8 workgroups takes 1.12 us; by all 304 at once 3.24 us (self-contention: this, not background traffic, is most of fleet's 5 us prologues). A second workgroup per CU streaming HBM at up to 3.34 TB/s leaves the fold at 3.24-3.36 us (x1.00-1.04). Streaming on the other CUs of an XCD instead doubles a small fold group (1.52 -> 3.08 us at 4.31 TB/s). The Infinity Cache is not faster than HBM for any expert-sized stream (10-192 MB), so prefetching weights into it buys nothing.

[380] docs/MOE-BODY-ANATOMY-2026-10-11.md:272 PLAN-300TOKS:97 => docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:97
CLAIM: 6. Current down **does not straddle rows**: `per=12`, `spr=3`, hence whole-row ownership. The plan's generic warning that down *can* straddle (`PLAN-300TOKS:97`) is valid for other shapes, but cannot exclude this guarded shape (§2.1). No protected plan was edited.
97: What might be removable now is **empty partial zeroing and cross-wave gathering for a row proved to have one contributing wave**, redundant loop guards for fixed shapes, and a duplicate barrier whose LDS lifetime/visibility proof permits removal. Specialise Qwen's proven GU1 row mapping, leave the wave sum and bf16 rounding identical, write gate/up row results directly to their existing LDS locations, then retain the barrier joining gate and up before SiLU. This may avoid gathering zeros from other waves. Down can straddle wave ownership and must keep its required partial order; it cannot inherit the up proof blindly (`gemv.py:183-184,272-275,768-775`). Do not omit real stores, skip the SiLU, reassociate fp32 sums, or replace payload visibility with a relaxed fence.

[386] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:9 docs/PLAN-generic-decoder-2026-10-08.md:154 => docs/PLAN-generic-decoder-2026-10-08.md:154
CLAIM: - `rebase.py` applies the plan's own lever rule (B = 48 x (critical body - round11 serial floor), `docs/PLAN-generic-decoder-2026-10-08.md:154`) to three traces. On `tr_all` it reproduces every plan allowance exactly: 0.086/0.112/0.125/0.123/0.168/0.324/0.139/0.019 ms (`RB:5-13`, against `docs/PLAN-generic-decoder-2026-10-08.md:224-230`). That rule says a KEEP must be followed by "rebase every bound on the new trace" (`:154`). Nobody did this after KEY32. `RB` does it.
154: **Lever rule.** (1) *Bound.* Every lever states B in ms/token on the current reference trace (today `results_tiles/tr_all.bin`, build `all`). For body levers, B = 48 x (critical body - serial floor) of the phases it changes (`etx/tools/floors.py`). For queue levers, B = 48 x (start delay - dispatcher jitter). A bound on an older trace does not count. (2) *Pursue.* B >= 0.050: build it, CPU gates first. 0.030 <= B < 0.050: build only as a passenger in a session already planned. B < 0.030: closed without a build, because an A/B cannot resolve it. (3) *Test.* At least 3 interleaved pairs on one GPU, bit-identical output or 32/32 + CHECK, device median, with wall printed beside it. The verdict follows `examples/vllm_llm/tile_ab.py`: KEEP when every pair is faster and the mean is <= -0.030; NEUTRAL when |mean| < 0.030; SLOWER at >= +0.030; MIXED otherwise. (4) *Act.* KEEP: make it a default in the next commit, then rebase every bound on the new trace. NEUTRAL or SLOWER: close it and record it in the settled list. MIXED: one more session with 5 pairs, then close it unless KEEP. At most two GPU sessions per lever. (5) *The 0.030-0.050 band.* A KEEP in this band is real on the device basis (above the 0.017-0.028 same-binary spread, `examples/vllm_llm/tile_ab.py:25`) but below the 0.050 system threshold (`docs/SPEC-nv-reference-2026-10-08.md:167`). Ship it, but do not quote it against vLLM on its own; only the sum of kept levers, measured in one session against vLLM, is quoted.

[398] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:25 docs/FLOORS-2026-10-08.md:319 => docs/FLOORS-2026-10-08.md:319
CLAIM: Decided by: `docs/FLOORS-2026-10-08.md:319`, with `docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:66`.
319: The argument prefetch `ETX_VL_ARGPF` was measured as neutral, -0.003 ms. `ETX_VL_BAR2` was the build that was 0.095 ms slower. BAR2 raised the LDS of the megakernel from 41,448 B to 61,952 B. Source: `docs/RESULTS-2026-10-01.md:97-98`. Explain these two results before a new prefetch design. The cause of the BAR2 result is not known. Occupancy does not explain it: the workgroup has 1024 threads and 128 VGPRs, so each CU holds 1 workgroup with or without BAR2, and 61,952 B is less than 64 KB. Thus BAR2 is not evidence against LDS staging for prefetch. The ISA comparison is in `/workspace/isa_r11/BAR2_cmp/README.md`. The +0.095 ms belongs to the round 2 pair (tile `79d4b4f`, topk fold on, 59,978 tasks). MATCH with BAR2 was never measured. In that pair, BAR2 has fewer spills (13 to 10) and fewer scratch accesses (21 to 13). The tile-body `ds_*` instructions and offsets are identical, so bank conflicts do not change. The extra 20,504 B of LDS is 20,480 B plus 24 B. The compiler (promote-alloca) put `nx_task` in LDS, 20 B per lane for 1024 lanes. The other 24 B is the second parity slot. In the base build, thread 0 loads the next task descriptor (3 `global_load`) before the event wait, so the wait hides the load. In the BAR2 build, these loads come after the event wait and before the body, followed by `s_waitcnt vmcnt(0)`. This adds one round trip before each task body. Hypothesis, not verified: this exposed round trip causes the +0.095 ms. The denominator is the critical-path task count from `trace_r2_base.bin`: 388 tasks, joined by 387 dependency edges and 0 same-worker queue edges. 0.095 ms / 388 is about 0.24 us per critical task. BAR2 also removed 2 barriers, about 0.02 ms each (RESULTS). With these added back, the gross cost is about 0.135 ms / 388, about 0.35 us. Both values are less than one full round trip (0.50 to 0.69 us), so the data supports one extra load per critical task with part of its round trip exposed. No BAR2 trace exists in `results_2026-10-01/round2/` (only `trace_r2_base.bin`), so a new BAR2 traced run is necessary to verify it.

[399] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:25 docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:66 => docs/PLAN-300TOKS-2026-10-11.md:66
66: Keep the current head-affine parallel partitions. Assign an owner for each `(step, layer, kv_head)`, with exactly one prep execution. The owner performs the existing q/k norm and RoPE, preserves its bf16 rounding and writes the current KV row once. After **all owner waves' stores have drained**, publish a head-local, epoch-qualified `prep_ready`. Publish it **before** the owner executes its attention partition, not at the end of the whole task. The other partitions acquire that publication and run their unchanged imported attention calls in parallel. The owner also runs its own partition.

Occupancy does not explain it: the workgroup has 1024 threads and 128 VGPRs, so each CU holds 1 workgroup with or without BAR2, and 61,952 B is less than 64 KB. Thus BAR2 is not evidence against LDS staging for prefetch. The ISA comparison is in `/workspace/isa_r11/BAR2_cmp/README.md`. The +0.095 ms belongs to the round 2 pair (tile `79d4b4f`, topk fold on, 59,978 tasks). MATCH with BAR2 was never measured. In that pair, BAR2 has fewer spills (13 to 10) and fewer scratch accesses (21 to 13). The tile-body `ds_*` instructions and offsets are identical, so bank conflicts do not change. The extra 20,504 B of LDS is 20,480 B plus 24 B. The compiler (promote-alloca) put `nx_task` in LDS, 20 B per lane for 1024 lanes. The other 24 B is the second parity slot. In the base build, thread 0 loads the next task descriptor (3 `global_load`) before the event wait, so the wait hides the load. In the BAR2 build, these loads come after the event wait and before the body, followed by `s_waitcnt vmcnt(0)`. This adds one round trip before each task body. Hypothesis, not verified: this exposed round trip causes the +0.095 ms. The denominator is the critical-path task count from `trace_r2_base.bin`: 388 tasks, joined by 387 dependency edges and 0 same-worker queue edges. 0.095 ms / 388 is about 0.24 us per critical task. BAR2 also removed 2 barriers, about 0.02 ms each (RESULTS). With these added back, the gross cost is about 0.135 ms / 388, about 0.35 us. Both values are less than one full round trip (0.50 to 0.69 us), so the data supports one extra load per critical task with part of its round trip exposed. No BAR2 trace exists in `results_2026-10-01/round2/` (only `trace_r2_base.bin`), so a new BAR2 traced run is necessary to verify it.

[406] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:33 docs/XCD-TIMELINE-2026-10-11.md:281 => docs/XCD-TIMELINE-2026-10-11.md:281
281: The per-phase bandwidth shares above divide each phase's achieved GB/s by the whole device's 4.3 TB/s. No phase runs on all eight XCDs. `latency.txt` already measures the per-phase `XCDs live` column (`docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/latency.txt:5-14`). One XCD's share of the achievable 4.3 TB/s is `4.3 / 8 = 0.5375 TB/s` [E], so a phase's own ceiling is `XCDs live x 0.5375 TB/s`, and its share of that ceiling is the only bandwidth number that means anything for it. A phase on four XCDs could never have reached 4.3 TB/s; comparing it to 4.3 TB/s makes latency-bound phases look like they waste bandwidth they never had.

[415] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:39 XT:324 => docs/XCD-TIMELINE-2026-10-11.md:324
324: One precision about what "idle" means here. `any body` marks an XCD busy when at least one of its 38 workers is in a task body. At worker granularity the busy share is lower: bodies cover 40.03% of worker-time (bytes 27.57% + machinery 12.46%, `account.txt:8-9`), and 53.00% of worker-time is dependency wait (`account.txt:6-7`). A worker in a dependency wait has already taken its next task and still occupies its CU; the 27.06% counts XCDs with no body at all, where all 38 workers are waiting or done. Both numbers matter below: the first says how much work could in principle be re-issued, the second says how much of the machine has nothing issued at all.

278:         n = name.lower()

280:         if 'ck_moe_stage2' in n: return 'stage2'
281:         if 'reduce_segments' in n or ('reduce' in n and ('attn' in n or 'attention' in n)): return 'reduce'
282:         if 'attn' in n or 'attention' in n:

289:         # Either category participates in prefill (48), or decode only (0).

291:             raise RuntimeError(f'{precision} {category}: NOT-COMPLETE coverage short={len(a)} long={len(b)} expected difference=1488; no dispatch workaround')
292:         decode = b[len(a):]
293:         for layer in range(48):
294:             samples = [(e-s)/1000 for s, e, _ in decode[layer::48]]
295:             if len(samples) != 31 or min(samples) <= 0:
296:                 raise RuntimeError('invalid layer samples')

[473] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:128 FLOORS:319 => docs/FLOORS-2026-10-08.md:319
319: The argument prefetch `ETX_VL_ARGPF` was measured as neutral, -0.003 ms. `ETX_VL_BAR2` was the build that was 0.095 ms slower. BAR2 raised the LDS of the megakernel from 41,448 B to 61,952 B. Source: `docs/RESULTS-2026-10-01.md:97-98`. Explain these two results before a new prefetch design. The cause of the BAR2 result is not known. Occupancy does not explain it: the workgroup has 1024 threads and 128 VGPRs, so each CU holds 1 workgroup with or without BAR2, and 61,952 B is less than 64 KB. Thus BAR2 is not evidence against LDS staging for prefetch. The ISA comparison is in `/workspace/isa_r11/BAR2_cmp/README.md`. The +0.095 ms belongs to the round 2 pair (tile `79d4b4f`, topk fold on, 59,978 tasks). MATCH with BAR2 was never measured. In that pair, BAR2 has fewer spills (13 to 10) and fewer scratch accesses (21 to 13). The tile-body `ds_*` instructions and offsets are identical, so bank conflicts do not change. The extra 20,504 B of LDS is 20,480 B plus 24 B. The compiler (promote-alloca) put `nx_task` in LDS, 20 B per lane for 1024 lanes. The other 24 B is the second parity slot. In the base build, thread 0 loads the next task descriptor (3 `global_load`) before the event wait, so the wait hides the load. In the BAR2 build, these loads come after the event wait and before the body, followed by `s_waitcnt vmcnt(0)`. This adds one round trip before each task body. Hypothesis, not verified: this exposed round trip causes the +0.095 ms. The denominator is the critical-path task count from `trace_r2_base.bin`: 388 tasks, joined by 387 dependency edges and 0 same-worker queue edges. 0.095 ms / 388 is about 0.24 us per critical task. BAR2 also removed 2 barriers, about 0.02 ms each (RESULTS). With these added back, the gross cost is about 0.135 ms / 388, about 0.35 us. Both values are less than one full round trip (0.50 to 0.69 us), so the data supports one extra load per critical task with part of its round trip exposed. No BAR2 trace exists in `results_2026-10-01/round2/` (only `trace_r2_base.bin`), so a new BAR2 traced run is necessary to verify it.

[474] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:129 XT:281 => docs/XCD-TIMELINE-2026-10-11.md:281
CLAIM: 3. XT:281's premise is false (claim 4). XT section 8.2's comparison mixes three bases (claim 5).
281: The per-phase bandwidth shares above divide each phase's achieved GB/s by the whole device's 4.3 TB/s. No phase runs on all eight XCDs. `latency.txt` already measures the per-phase `XCDs live` column (`docs/xcd-timeline-2026-10-11/latency.txt:5-14`). One XCD's share of the achievable 4.3 TB/s is `4.3 / 8 = 0.5375 TB/s` [E], so a phase's own ceiling is `XCDs live x 0.5375 TB/s`, and its share of that ceiling is the only bandwidth number that means anything for it. A phase on four XCDs could never have reached 4.3 TB/s; comparing it to 4.3 TB/s makes latency-bound phases look like they waste bandwidth they never had.

[475] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:130 PF:69-72 => docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:69-72
CLAIM: 4. PF:69-72 uses a rate gain as a time saving (102.9/58.2 us, not 120.8/63.2). PF:76 and PF:121 apply a 4.31 TB/s interference figure to 0.67-2.0 TB/s prefetches. PF:45 and PF:123 apply loaded latency to lightly loaded phases.
69: The saving, if everything works, is the consumer phases' byte time re-priced at the cache-resident rate. Critical-path byte times [E]: o_proj 187.3, moe_down 280.9, qkv 234.1 us (`account.txt:43,44,47`), sum 702.3 us. The rate deltas are the measured ones of section 2:
70:
71: - **Upper bound: 120.8 us [E]** = `702.3 x 0.172`, using the most favourable measured residency delta (+17.2% at 192 MB, `overlap.log:8`). That is 2.7% of the 4412.1 us span and 10.6% of the 1138 us gap.

[476] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:130 PF:76 => docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:76
76: Why the realised value will be lower than even 63 us: (a) the +9% was measured with no co-running consumer stream; during P2/P3 the prefetch competes with the running phase for the same HBM, and the measured price of filling idle bandwidth next to a latency chain is a doubling of its round trip, 1.52 -> 3.08 us (`overlap.log:15-16`, split placement, background 4.31 TB/s) — the latency phases carry 396.4 us of exposed hops [E], so even a 10% slowdown there (~40 us) eats most of the central estimate; (b) the MALL window sits at 86% occupancy with the KV cache, so some eviction is likely and every evicted line is read twice (section 2); (c) if NT consumer loads cannot hit the prefetched lines [U], the consumers must go cacheable, risking the fleet 18% L2-cliff loss; (d) fleet already measured this mechanism's two direct ancestors on the same GPU: idle-worker prefetch into the Infinity Cache gave no gain, and prefetch across the event wait was 4% slower (fleet `docs/STATUS.md:72,30`).

[477] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:130 PF:121 => docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:121
121: 2. **Filling the idle ports has a measured price on the work that matters.** The fold benchmark on this GPU: the latency chain's round trip went 1.52 -> 3.08 us when a background stream filled the idle bandwidth (`overlap.log:15-16`). The latency phases carry 396.4 us of exposed hops [E]; a slowdown there is paid on the critical path, while the prefetch benefit is bounded at 63-121 us [E]. The mechanism can be net negative even when it works.

[478] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:130 PF:45 => docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:45
45: - Load *latency* does not depend on the level under load: the round trip is 0.73 / 0.71 / 0.69 us at L2 / MALL / HBM with 303 CUs streaming (`docs/MODEL-vllm-kernels.md:321`) [M]. Queueing sets it, not the cache level. So caching cannot shorten the 534.4 us of exposed load round trips on the critical path (`account.txt:55`), including the 138.0 us of hops before the first weight load in the streaming phases.

[479] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:130 PF:123 => docs/XCD-PREFETCH-2026-10-11.md:123
123: 4. **Caching cannot shorten the latency that dominates.** The loaded round trip is level-independent (`docs/MODEL-vllm-kernels.md:321`), so residency touches only bandwidth exposure, which section 4 bounds at 2.7% of the span in the most favourable case.

[480] docs/REVIEW-POOL-ANALYSIS-2026-10-11.md:131 docs/PLAN-generic-decoder-2026-10-08.md:154 => docs/PLAN-generic-decoder-2026-10-08.md:154
CLAIM: 5. No document followed the lever rule's "rebase every bound on the new trace" after KEY32's KEEP (`docs/PLAN-generic-decoder-2026-10-08.md:154`). PLAN:130-132 still carry tr_all allowances.
154: **Lever rule.** (1) *Bound.* Every lever states B in ms/token on the current reference trace (today `results_tiles/tr_all.bin`, build `all`). For body levers, B = 48 x (critical body - serial floor) of the phases it changes (`etx/tools/floors.py`). For queue levers, B = 48 x (start delay - dispatcher jitter). A bound on an older trace does not count. (2) *Pursue.* B >= 0.050: build it, CPU gates first. 0.030 <= B < 0.050: build only as a passenger in a session already planned. B < 0.030: closed without a build, because an A/B cannot resolve it. (3) *Test.* At least 3 interleaved pairs on one GPU, bit-identical output or 32/32 + CHECK, device median, with wall printed beside it. The verdict follows `examples/vllm_llm/tile_ab.py`: KEEP when every pair is faster and the mean is <= -0.030; NEUTRAL when |mean| < 0.030; SLOWER at >= +0.030; MIXED otherwise. (4) *Act.* KEEP: make it a default in the next commit, then rebase every bound on the new trace. NEUTRAL or SLOWER: close it and record it in the settled list. MIXED: one more session with 5 pairs, then close it unless KEEP. At most two GPU sessions per lever. (5) *The 0.030-0.050 band.* A KEEP in this band is real on the device basis (above the 0.017-0.028 same-binary spread, `examples/vllm_llm/tile_ab.py:25`) but below the 0.050 system threshold (`docs/SPEC-nv-reference-2026-10-08.md:167`). Ship it, but do not quote it against vLLM on its own; only the sum of kept levers, measured in one session against vLLM, is quoted.
